It falls back to unblocked CPQR when min(m,n) < 2·blocksize (auto threshold ~192), and its pivots differ from classical CPQR, so the selected columns could change. HQRRP/BQRRP papers show wins only at n in the thousands. At EDI's t×p (p ≲ 50) there is no gain. The redundant second QR and the varying-set caching are already tracked (v1.0.5 TODO-29, kk14 plan TODO-1/2).
- Batch U.1: column-pivoted Householder QR: no_faster_acceptable_found. Eigen's benchmark (fetched) shows HouseholderQR ≈ ColPivHouseholderQR ≈ COD at 8x8 and 10000x8, and ColPivQR ~5x slower than LLT-on-normal-equations. The faster options (unpivoted QR, Cholesky) are not rank-revealing, or they square the condition number. Randomized CPQR (BQRRP) only pays at large sizes and changes the pivots. Keep for matrix_rank_cpp, qr_reduce_full_rank_cpp and robust_ols_solve.
- Batch U.1: eigendecomposition pseudo-inverse: no_faster_acceptable_found. This is only a fallback in covariance_from_information after LDLT fails, and it is O(p³) with small p. SelfAdjointEigenSolver is the right rank-revealing tool for a symmetric matrix. COD/SVD pseudo-inverses use a different threshold semantics (they would change the answer on rank-deficient input) and are no faster for symmetric input. Not a hot path.
- Batch U.1: full-pivot LU inverse: no_faster_acceptable_found. No new option. Eigen's benchmark (fetched) quantifies the already-known gap: FullPivLU is 1.9x LLT at 8x8 and 10.6x at 100x100, while PartialPivLU is 1.5x and LDLT 1.3x. That supports the existing Keep-tracked item (performance_profiling_and_upgrades.md TODO-155: a single LDLT or reusing lu.inverse()). It is deliberately kept for bit-compatibility, and at EDI's p (≲ 20) the absolute cost is microseconds per fit, so this is not a new finding.
- Batch U.2: Gauss–Jordan elimination (partial pivoting): no_faster_acceptable_found. The faster option, Cholesky/LLT of (S + ridge) followed by whitening Z = X·L⁻ᵀ, is already adopted (catalog §8 TODO-1, folded into v1.0.5 TODO-29). The Eigen decomposition table (fetched) rates LLT 'very fast' and LU 'fast', which agrees. I found nothing new beyond that existing Adopt verdict.
- Batch U.2: hash-map single pass: no_faster_acceptable_found. O(n·p) single pass, which is already optimal: every row has to be touched once. The silently skipped 3rd member is a correctness note, not a speed issue.
- Batch U.2: Householder QR projection: no_faster_acceptable_found. Runs once per design setup at EDI's n. The only improvement is keeping P = QQ' in low-rank form instead of n×n, which the inventory already records (relevant to TODO-1). Nothing new found.
- Batch U.2: LDLT inverse with eigen fallback: no_faster_acceptable_found. O(p³) at p ≲ 20, called once per fit. Eigen rates LDLT 'very fast'; plain LLT would save a constant factor at most and would change which inputs reach the pseudo-inverse fallback. Not worth it.
- Batch U.2: LDLT solve against unit vector: no_faster_acceptable_found. One factorization plus a single-RHS solve is already the cheap way to get one diagonal entry of M⁻¹. The alternatives (selected inversion, LLT) save only constants at small p and change the fallback behaviour on indefinite inputs.
- Batch U.3: linear scan for non-constant columns: no_faster_acceptable_found. which_cols_vary in compute_all_subject_data.cpp is an O(tp) early-exit scan that has to read each column at least until it finds the first differing value, so nothing asymptotically faster exists. The real cost in that row is the duplicate ColPivHouseholderQR (pivot pass, then a second QR only for rank), which is already tracked as v1.0.5 TODO-29 'drop the redundant QR' and kk14_incremental_covariance.md TODO-1/2.
- Batch U.3: linear scan with early exit: no_faster_acceptable_found. which_cols_vary_cpp is an O(np) worst-case compare against row 0 that exits early, which is the lower bound for this problem. It also has no R caller outside RcppExports.
- Batch U.3: linear-pass summary statistics: no_faster_acceptable_found. The KK pair-difference and reservoir statistics (compute_zhang_match_data_cpp, compute_matching_lin_match_data_cpp, compute_matching_lin_wy_stats_cpp, and the R wrappers) are one or two O(np) or O(n) scatter passes, and reading the data is already the lower bound. The X-structural part is cached per m_vec, and the wy-only variant already skips X on redraws. No algorithmic alternative applies.
- Batch U.3: Monte Carlo pair sampling (distance quantile): no_faster_acceptable_found. At O(Bd) with B = 500 per arrival this is already trivial. Exact all-pairs enumeration when t(t-1)/2 <= B is the only alternative, and it is already a tracked Prototype (catalog §7, TODO-7; v1.0.5 TODO-35). It changes design draws for a given seed, which makes it a design-rule change rather than a speedup. I found no newer selection or incomplete-U-statistic result for weighted multivariate pair distances that would beat O(Bd) at EDI's sizes. Fetching the KK21 arXiv PDF to confirm the paper's threshold definition failed (binary PDF, no text extraction available), so that point is unverified.
- Batch U.3: pivoted QR rank detection: no_faster_acceptable_found. drop_linearly_dependent_cols runs the second R LINPACK qr only when the matrix is rank-deficient, so the common full-rank path costs one factorization. On the Eigen benchmark page, ColPivHouseholderQR is about 4.3x LLT at 8x8 and the same speed as CompleteOrthogonalDecomposition and FullPivHouseholderQR, so no faster rank-revealing Eigen decomposition exists. A pivoted Cholesky of X'X would be faster but squares the condition number, which fails the accuracy criterion. The 'one pivoted QR would do' cleanup and the tolerance mismatch are already recorded in catalog §1. I could not confirm that Eigen 5.0 added blocking to ColPivHouseholderQR, and it would not matter at EDI's p.
- Batch U.3: stable sort + run-length grouping: no_faster_acceptable_found. This is a one-time O(n log n) layout step. A stable counting sort on dense integer group ids would be O(n + G), but the catalog already records it as immaterial next to the O(n·n_gh) GLMM objective evaluated many times per fit. Nothing new.
- Batch U.4: unblocked weighted Gram accumulation: no_faster_acceptable_found. The one known faster option is forming √w∘X and calling DSYRK, or Eigen selfadjointView().rankUpdate, which the BigDataStatMeth xwxt code also uses (https://isglobal-brge.github.io/BigDataStatMeth/api-reference/cpp/functions/xwxt.html). That option is already in catalog P7 and tracked as performance_profiling_and_upgrades.md TODO-155. I found no benchmark at EDI's sizes (small p, n in the hundreds to low thousands) dated after the 2026-10-22 review. The Aug-2025 numpy 'weighted gram / sandwich product' proposal reports no timings and confirms the √w trade-offs already listed: an extra n·p temporary, square roots, and w ≥ 0 required (https://mail.python.org/archives/list/numpy-discussion@python.org/thread/AI3VFPYTVAQTHQOZAYDXI6RSCACJC2YV/). tabmat's benchmarks are at 4M×10 and give no numbers on the page. The CRAN release of RcppEigen is still 0.3.4.0.2 (2024-08-24, https://cran.r-project.org/package=RcppEigen), so Eigen 5.0.x (Sep/Nov 2025, https://libeigen.gitlab.io/) changes nothing for EDI yet. Keep-tracked stands.
- Batch U.4: two-pass mean/variance: no_faster_acceptable_found. scale_columns_cpp computes a column mean and SD in O(np), which is memory-bound. A one-pass Σx² form saves one read but loses accuracy through cancellation. Welford is slower per element and, per arXiv 2410.21922 (fetched in search results), less accurate than two-pass at large offsets. The function also has no R caller. Nothing faster that is also acceptable.
- Batch U.4: stacked design assembly: no_faster_acceptable_found. build_matching_combined_{clogit,ols}_design_cpp write each output element once, O((n_d+n_R)·p), which is the output-size lower bound. The OLS builder has no production caller. The open items are statistical, as noted in the catalog's Incidental findings, not speed.
- Batch V.1: jackknife bias and variance (Quenouille–Tukey): no_faster_acceptable_found. Closed-form moments of the jackknife vector, O(n_units) after the replicates are computed. Nothing faster exists. The unequal-unit weighting issue (I17) is an accuracy item that is already recorded.
- Batch V.1: BCa bootstrap (jackknife acceleration): no_faster_acceptable_found. The z0, â and quantile steps are O(B log B), so the cost is in the jackknife refits. bcaboot::bcajack (CRAN, GPL-2/3) groups the n rows into m groups (typically 20 or 40) and averages mr = 5 random groupings. That is the catalog's 'grouped jackknife [cite needed]' option, and bcaboot can fill the citation. It costs about m·mr = 100–200 fits, so it only beats delete-one when n_units is above about 200. It changes â (randomly), so the earlier rejection stands. For least-squares statistics, the exact closed-form jackknife (see the delete-one finding) removes the refits without changing â. The influence-function option is already v1.0.5 TODO-7.
- Batch V.1: double bootstrap calibration: no_faster_acceptable_found. Chang & Hall (Biometrika 2015, arXiv 1408.6327) show that single-inner-sample ('fast' or warp-speed) double bootstraps give third-order gains only for bias correction. Those gains are not available for confidence intervals, so cutting the inner bootstrap to one resample is not acceptable for this CI calibration. The prepivoting rewrite already planned (v1.0.5 TODO-2, about 5× fewer refits) is still the best option.
- Batch V.1: Monte Carlo Bartlett correction: no_faster_acceptable_found. The literature I found (Rocke 1989, via arXiv 1501.07551; Birnbaum–Saunders and beta-regression studies) supports a mean-of-LR bootstrap factor at B ≈ 100. Nothing newer offers a cheaper estimator of E[LR|H0] that keeps the per-δ estimator. The audit's options (one factor per CI, analytic Cordeiro-type factors; v1.0.5 TODO-3) still cover the space.
- Batch V.1: cold start (warm start disabled): no_faster_acceptable_found. The path-following and predictor–corrector literature I found (arXiv 0908.0143, 2307.05109) reports only that tangent predictors and warm starts cut corrector iterations, with no profile-likelihood benchmark. One source even finds a plain warm start as good as the tangent predictor. Nothing beyond the deterministic tangent predictor already planned (v1.0.5 TODO-5) turned up.
- Batch V.1: minimum-volatility method: no_faster_acceptable_found. In moonboot (arXiv 2412.05032), Bickel–Sakov costs O(Rn) and Götze–Račkauskas O(Rn²) by exhaustive search, so neither is cheaper than EDI's 9-point grid. moonboot does report poor coverage for Politis minimum volatility, but that is with a search up to m < n/2, where m is effectively proportional to n. EDI's n^0.5…n^0.9 grid keeps m/n → 0, so the criticism may not carry over. It is an accuracy and estimator question, not a speed one, and worth a look under the existing Bickel–Sakov option.
- Batch V.1: fixed-replicate Monte Carlo with hash-set dedupe: no_faster_acceptable_found. The FNV-1a uint64 unordered_set dedupe is O(1) per key and negligible next to the fits. The precision-targeted Nrep is already option (n) in catalog §12. Warp-speed Monte Carlo (Giacomini, Politis & White 2013) could only cut bootstrap-CI cells, it changes what is measured per replicate (no per-replicate CI), and I could not fetch its cost or limitation details, so it is not reported as a finding.
- Batch V.2: Monte Carlo design variance: no_faster_acceptable_found. The faster option is the closed-form Var(y'w) from the design's second moments: O(n), deterministic, exact for CR/BCRD/Bernoulli/blocked designs. The catalog already records it and the row's verdict is already Prototype (v1.0.5 TODO-3). The code still draws K=5000 (inference_incidence_cmh.R:196). Nothing newer or faster exists for sequential designs, which still need MC.
- Batch V.2: pair extraction loop: no_faster_acceptable_found. The cost is an R loop running which(m_vec==pid) once per pair, O(n·m) per replicate. A single split()/match grouping does the same in O(n). This is an implementation fix the catalog already records as Incidental, not a new algorithm, and there is nothing to find in the literature.
- Batch V.2: parallel L-BFGS refit loop: no_faster_acceptable_found. compute_stereotype_logit_distr_parallel_cpp is dead code: it is referenced only in RcppExports.R (grep-verified at fa2fe36d) and has no R caller. A speed review does not apply; the action is to remove or rewire it, as the inventory already says.
- Batch V.2: parallel Newton refit loop: no_faster_acceptable_found. The adjacent-category likelihood is concave, so Newton converges quadratically and the answer does not depend on the start. Each draw starts from zero (fast_adjacent_category_logit.cpp:598). A cheaper deterministic start exists: the closed-form intercept-only MLE α_k=log(n_{k+1}/n_k) with β=0, which is close under the permutation null. It would save at most ~1–2 of a handful of Newton iterations. I found no source quantifying this, so it is not filed as a finding.
- Batch V.2: reused-worker refit loop: no_faster_acceptable_found. For Lin and KK OLS, permuting w changes the interaction columns, so the FWL/Freedman–Lane residual tricks do not apply. Each draw stays O(n p²); the recorded C++ batch-kernel Prototype is the right fix, and the search found no closed-form shortcut. Quantile regression: quantreg's ChangeLog through 6.01 adds no new fast or warm-startable exact solver (CRAN 6.1 is dated 2025-03-10), and br remains fastest at EDI's small n. PPO/VGAM: nothing new. Robust: the recorded batch-C++ Prototype stands.
- Batch V.2: warm-start continuation (chained): no_faster_acceptable_found. The faster, path-independent option is the deterministic tangent predictor θ̂ + (δ−θ̂_j)[−I_nn⁻¹I_nj; 1]. It is already in the catalog (NEW-ci-refit-warm-start, audit TODO-5). The search found nothing newer for these 20–25-refit-per-bound searches.
- Batch V.2: weighted delete-one jackknife: no_faster_acceptable_found. The alternatives (infinitesimal jackknife / empirical influence, ABC, regression-IJ, grouped jackknife, one-step approximate LOO) are all already in the catalog with their estimator-change caveats, and a search turned up nothing new from 2024–2026. The serial vapply could run through par_lapply, but that is parallelisation, not an algorithm change.
- Batch W.1: Barrodale–Roberts simplex (quantreg "br"): no_faster_acceptable_found. quantreg 6.1 (CRAN, 2025-03-10) ChangeLog has no solver or speed changes since the row was reviewed. fn/pfn/sfn only win at n ≫ 10^3 or with sparse X, and conquer/smoothed QR changes the estimator, so all were already rejected. Chernozhukov–Fernández-Val–Melly 2022 also find no preprocessing gain for a single fit at n=500 (k=8). Keep br; the open Adopt item (rq.fit instead of formula rq) still stands.
- Batch W.1: Frisch–Newton interior point / Barrodale–Roberts simplex: no_faster_acceptable_found. No new evidence. At EDI's n, br and fn run at about the same speed (fn 0.02 s at n=500 in CFM 2022, Table 1). The open issue is consistency: the point fit uses fn while the BB replicates use br, so they can return different solutions when the solution is non-unique. That is a correctness issue, not a speed one, and the existing Prototype (one method for both paths) is unchanged. See the preprocessing finding for the BB replicates.
- Batch W.1: Hodges–Lehmann estimator: no_faster_acceptable_found. stats::wilcox.test runs an exact sorted-Walsh path only for m<50 and uses the normal approximation with uniroot otherwise, which is cheap at EDI sizes. Monahan 1984 and Johnson–Mizoguchi are already in the catalog and pay off only at n in the thousands; the NAG and DescTools implementations found add nothing newer. The open item here is the SE scale (I2/F7), a correctness bug, not a speed one.
- Batch W.1: Hodges–Lehmann estimator (selection): no_faster_acceptable_found. EDI materializes the differences and runs introselect when n_T·n_C ≤ 4096, otherwise sorts and uses value-space bisection with two-pointer counts. The only asymptotically better options are Johnson–Mizoguchi/Monahan O(n log n) selection, already catalogued. No newer algorithm or benchmark found; the planned hoisting and early-stop TODOs remain the right levers.
- Batch W.1: induced-smoothing Gehan rank estimation: no_faster_acceptable_found. aftgee 1.2.1 (2024-10-07, GPL≥3) is still the current CRAN release. No source found an O(n log n) evaluation of the smoothed Gehan equation; it is O(n²) per pair sum. The ADMM penAFT approach (arXiv 2104.01707) solves the non-smooth Gehan loss, which is a different estimator, and is still quadratic in n. The dominant cost is the F22 bug (B=0 gives an NA SE, so all 13 ladder rungs are fitted and rejected), not the algorithm.
- Batch W.1: introselect (std::nth_element): no_faster_acceptable_found. Floyd–Rivest selection in miniselect (Boost License, GPL-compatible) is claimed faster than libstdc++ nth_element, but only at 2^16 and 10^7 elements, with no published number at EDI's ≤4096 sizes (ClickHouse reported a 2.5% overall gain). It is a constant-factor change on an O(N) step that is dominated by materializing the differences. Not worth a dependency.
- Batch W.1: quantile regression (via component): no_faster_acceptable_found. Thin wrapper classes with no algorithm of their own; the solver is reviewed under the br and sign-flip entries of the owning components.
- Batch W.2: value-space bisection selection (two-pointer count): no_faster_acceptable_found. This is the hot path: per-replicate HL in compute_wilcox_hl_rand_bootstrap_parallel_cpp. Each bisection step is an O(n_T+n_C) two-pointer count with no extra memory, which is hard to beat at EDI sizes (n up to a few thousand). The asymptotically better options do not change that. Deterministic X+Y / sorted-matrix selection (Johnson–Mizoguchi, Frederickson–Johnson, and the simpler soft-heap version by Kaplan, Kozma, Zamir and Zwick, SOSA 2019, arXiv 1802.07041) only matches Frederickson–Johnson's O(n log n) bound for the median, with large constants. Its abstract (arXiv 1802.07041, fetched) gives no running time for selection in X+Y and no benchmark. The catalog already rejects this family. robslopes (CRAN 1.1.4, 2025-12-19, GPL-2|3) gets quasilinear medians of pairwise quantities with randomized Matoušek / Dillencourt-style algorithms. Those consume randomness, so they would need a private deterministic pivot to keep R streams untouched; that is the same trade-off as the catalog's Monahan rank-space option. The article reports speedups only against brute-force O(n²) implementations, not against an O(n·L) bisection. Nothing newer than the row's 2026-11-16 review overturns its verdict. The tracked wilcox_hl_kernel_hoisting.md TODO-3 (early stop, with the tie-safe criterion from Incidental 6) and TODO-4 (warm bracket) remain the right speedups. Same verdict for the one-sample Walsh-average variants (count_walsh_avgs_leq, max_walsh_avg_leq, select_walsh_avg_sorted), whose only caller is the legacy IVWC path.
- Batch W.2: weighted Hodges–Lehmann estimator: no_faster_acceptable_found. The weighted branch of hl_point_estimate (inference_all_simple_wilcox.R:256-279) appears to be unreachable at fa2fe36d. The class declares supports_bayesian_bootstrap = function() FALSE (line 231), and grep finds no caller that passes row_weights: all three call sites (lines 319, 341, 369) pass only (y, w). So there is no hot path to speed up. The inventory row describing it as 'weighted HL for the Bayesian bootstrap' looks stale and should be re-checked. If the branch is ever revived, there is a faster route that gives the same estimator. With product weights w_i·v_j, the weighted count of diffs ≤ x is Σ_j v_j · (prefix sum of w over y_T ≤ x + y_Cj). That can be computed with the existing two-pointer sweep plus prefix sums in O(n_T+n_C), so the existing value-space bisection and snap give the lower weighted median (smallest realized diff with cumulative weight ≥ 1/2, as the current code returns) in O(n log n + n·L). The current code does an O(n_T·n_C·log) outer+order in R. The answer matches up to floating-point summation order at an exact 1/2 boundary. This is not quantified by any source: I found no published weighted-X+Y-median algorithm (searches turned up only the sort-or-select weighted median, https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Weighted_median). It is not worth doing while the branch is dead.
- Batch X.1: closed form (Huber sandwich): no_faster_acceptable_found. Closed form, O(n + p³) using the already-cached OLS R factor, so nothing is faster. The known issues (no guard for mean ψ′→0; small-sample anti-conservatism per Koller & Stahel 2011) are about accuracy and estimators, not speed, and are already recorded (Incidental 10, catalog O8).
- Batch X.1: introselect (median): no_faster_acceptable_found. O(n) nth_element (with sort below n=512) for the MAD median, computed once per fit. Catalog O9 already rejects Floyd–Rivest at n≤10⁴, and I found no newer selection result that would matter for a once-per-fit median at EDI's sizes.
- Batch X.1: IRLS M/MM-estimation: no_faster_acceptable_found. These R-level entries call the C++ IRLS above, with MASS::rlm as a fallback. robustbase NEWS 2023–2026 has no algorithmic speedup for lmrob/FAST-S beyond lmrob.fit(bare.only=), a reduced-output option. The KK classes use Huber 'M' with maxit capped at 10–25, and they would benefit from finding (1) on the C++ kernel, where it would make convergence exact. No separate R-level change is needed.
- Batch Y.1: ICM + Newton (icenReg ic_sp) + bootstrap SE: no_faster_acceptable_found. icenReg's JSS paper shows ic_sp converging in 11 iterations (0.023 s) at n=500, against 25 s for intcox at strict tolerance. Its fitter is ICM plus a constrained gradient-ascent step plus conditional Newton. The 200-refit bootstrap SE has only one alternative: the profile likelihood already listed in the catalog. ICsurv's Hessian/Louis SE uses a spline baseline, which is an estimator change. EDI's resampling loops already pass bs_samples=0 when only the estimate is needed. icenReg NEWS shows no new SE method.
- Batch Y.1: log-rank test (sorted risk-set sweep): no_faster_acceptable_found. Right-censored path: one sort plus a linear sweep, O(n log n), in C++. Nothing faster exists for an exact statistic. The interval-censored variant is covered by the NPMLE rows.
- Batch Z.1: active-set projection (Fisher metric): no_faster_acceptable_found. Reuses the iteration's LDLT, and the extra cost O(|A|p²+|A|³) is negligible next to the O(np²) IRLS step. A conic or QP solver would replace the whole scheme rather than speed up this step (see the IRLS finding).
- Batch Z.1: KKT conditions check: no_faster_acceptable_found. A small multiplier solve per check that reuses the LDLT. Nothing cheaper certifies a boundary MLE. An interior-point conic solver would return certified dual multipliers directly (see the IRLS finding).
- Batch Z.1: Karush-Kuhn-Tucker boundary score and information reduction: no_faster_acceptable_found. O(|C|p³) with tiny |C| and p, run once per test, so cost is not an issue. Side note, not speed: the loop drops every negative multiplier at once rather than following Lawson–Hanson NNLS. That can pick a different active set when constraints are degenerate. Worth a correctness look; not reported as a speed finding.
- Batch Z.1: bisection on the score: no_faster_acceptable_found. The already-adopted closed-form cubic (v1.0.5 TODO-3 in the audit, Farrington–Manning 1990, cited by CRAN exact2x2's constrMLE.difference) is still the faster option. No new evidence changes that verdict, and nothing faster than O(1) exists. Anderson (arXiv 2205.03460) gives closed forms for the extreme cases, which matter for the cubic's root choice.
- Batch Z.1: g-computation (closed form) + drop-largest-coefficient: no_faster_acceptable_found. The plogis averages are a closed form, O(np), and nothing is faster. The only alternative to the separation fallback is Firth, which the catalog already rejected as an estimator change.
- Batch Z.1: gradient-norm acceptance threshold: no_faster_acceptable_found. An O(1) threshold heuristic; there is nothing to speed up.
- Batch Z.1: boundary detection + reduced-model refit (L-BFGS): no_faster_acceptable_found. Costs one extra NB fit and only on the boundary path. The bound-constrained parametrization alternative is already tracked in v1.0.5 TODO-32 (catalog §3 TODO-4). No newer method found.
- Batch Z.2: quasi-binomial IRLS + grid search on log φ: no_faster_acceptable_found. Hot path (one fit per KK21 candidate covariate, per arrival). The only faster option known is Brent or safeguarded Newton on the 1-D log φ profile, using the digamma score and the trigamma curvature that betareg already uses: about 19 → 5–8 loglik-equivalents. It is already recorded in catalog P4 and tracked as a Prototype (v1.0.5 TODO 4), so it is not re-reported. betareg NEWS (https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/betareg/news/news.html) adds no new faster φ method, only the Ferrari–Cribari-Neto start and a Fisher-scoring polish. Switching the mean model to the full beta MLE is an estimator change and is slower.
- Batch Z.2: quadratic soft barrier penalty: no_faster_acceptable_found. O(1) per evaluation, so it adds no cost. The alternatives listed in catalog T4 (L-BFGS-B, lme4-style σ≥0 bounds, glmmTMB priors) are not faster and change the boundary behaviour. lme4 2.1-0 (2026-10-23) NEWS only changes how zero variances are reported, and glmmTMB 1.1.15.2 NEWS has no boundary or optimizer change: https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/lme4/news.html https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/glmmTMB/news.html. The Prototype (low) remains about accuracy and flat gradients, not speed.
- Batch Z.2: method of moments (start): no_faster_acceptable_found. Closed-form start: an O(np²) QR followed by an O(n) moment α̂. The QR is bounded below by reading the data, and the start does not change the MLE. Nothing faster.
- Batch Z.2: OLS on transformed response: no_faster_acceptable_found. These are glm mustart-style closed-form starts costing one O(np²) least-squares solve each. Nothing cheaper exists. Better starts (consistent truncated-count ZIP start, intercept fixes) are about iteration count and are already tracked (v1.0.5 TODO-24, multistart and EM plans), not new.
- Batch Z.2: magnitude + quantile-width separation heuristic: no_faster_acceptable_found. O(B) scan plus one type-8 quantile, run once per bootstrap distribution rather than per replicate, so it is negligible. The exact alternative is LP separation detection per refit (detectseparation 0.4.0, 2026-04-19, Konis 2007 LPs: https://cran.r-project.org/package=detectseparation). That is far more expensive per replicate and answers a different, per-fit question, so it is not a speedup.
- Batch Z.2: Smithson–Verkuilen squeeze: no_faster_acceptable_found. Closed form, O(n), nothing faster. The newer alternative for 0/1 responses, betareg 3.2-0's extended-support XBX beta regression (https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/betareg/news/news.html), is an estimator change and costs more per fit (it adds a mixture parameter), so it is not a speedup.
- Batch Z.2: reduced-model refit (L-BFGS): no_faster_acceptable_found. It runs only on the θ→∞ fallback path (one extra fit). The known way to remove it is the α=1/θ bound-constrained reparametrization (v1.0.5 TODO 4), which is already tracked. The search found no new ZINB Poisson-limit estimation method that would be faster.
- Batch Z.2: limit-score boundary certification: no_faster_acceptable_found. Costs 3 objective/score evaluations and runs only on the boundary path. Nothing faster preserves the certificate. If the α=1/θ reparametrization (v1.0.5 TODO 4) is adopted, it would make this check unnecessary rather than faster.
- Batch AA.1: bivariate-normal censoring likelihood (closed form): no_faster_acceptable_found. Closed form per row, O(np) per evaluation, using log Φ and the Mills ratio. Nothing asymptotically faster exists. Comparator packages (SemiPar.depCens, depCensoring 0.1.11 on CRAN, 2026-10-04) fit by pseudo-likelihood or semiparametric methods, which change the estimator rather than speed up this one.
- Batch AA.1: Clayton copula likelihood (closed form): no_faster_acceptable_found. Closed-form four-case pair likelihood, O(np) per evaluation, with overflow-safe ratios. The literature found offers only estimator changes (two-stage IFM, sieve or semiparametric methods), not a faster way to compute the same likelihood.
- Batch AA.1: column location/scale heuristic: no_faster_acceptable_found. One O(np) pass per column, about the cost of reading X once, and it runs once per fit. Nothing faster is possible. The separate 0/1 scan could be fused into the mean pass, but that is a negligible constant factor.
- Batch AA.1: cumulative-softmax reparametrization: no_faster_acceptable_found. O(K²) Jacobian once per likelihood evaluation, with K ≤ about 10. The O(nK) likelihood dominates it, so a prefix-sum O(K) form would not change wall-clock time.
- Batch AA.1: finite-difference profile curvature: no_faster_acceptable_found. Fallback only, used when the observed-information inverse is not finite: 3 profile refits. The obvious alternative (a Schur complement of the observed information) is exactly what failed when the fallback fires, so nothing cheaper is available. Not a hot path.
- Batch AA.1: multistart L-BFGS (BFGS / Nelder–Mead fallback): no_faster_acceptable_found. Clayton–Weibull AFT: 3 θ starts with C++ L-BFGS; the R fallbacks fire only on failure. Searches found no newer fast algorithm for Clayton-copula AFT MLE (Copula.surv and CopulaCenR use other estimators). L-BFGS-B and a 1-D θ profile were already considered in the catalog and have about the same cost.
- Batch AA.1: multistart L-BFGS (BFGS fallback): no_faster_acceptable_found. Dependent-censoring bivariate-lognormal model: 3 ρ starts with C++ L-BFGS and an analytic gradient. The FD-gradient optim fallback is slow, but it runs only on failure. Searches found no faster algorithm; packages from the same research group (SemiPar.depCens, depCensoring) use pseudo-likelihood or semiparametric estimators, which are estimator changes.
- Batch AA.2: Newton then L-BFGS fallback: no_faster_acceptable_found. The catalog (§5) already lists LM/trust-region Newton, damped Fisher scoring, alternating RR-VGLM fits and multistart, and v1.0.5 catalog TODO-6 tracks them. The only new method found is the MM algorithm of de Rooij (arXiv 2402.07634, v3 dated 2025-01-22), which the paper itself says has a linear rate and needs more iterations than IRLS, with no timings reported. VGAM 1.1-13 and 1.1-14 NEWS show no rrvglm/stereotype solver change; 1.1-14 only adds a 'bhhh' control option, with no claimed speedup. Nothing found that beats the current approach at EDI's sizes while keeping the same estimator.
- Batch AA.2: profile likelihood (damped Newton): no_faster_acceptable_found. Used only as a variance fallback: 3 profile refits when the observed-information diagonal inverse is not finite. It is warm-started from the MLE, and the analytic-Hessian damped Newton converges quadratically on a small nuisance block. The literature found (warm-start continuation, Newton with good starts) matches what the code already does. A possible micro-change, LDLT/LLT instead of FullPivLU on the small d×d system, is not algorithmic and not quantified.
- Batch AA.2: Newton decrement check (LDLT): no_faster_acceptable_found. A one-off post-fit certificate: one gradient, one Hessian and an O(p³/3) LDLT, run only when covariates look badly scaled. It is the standard affine-invariant stopping criterion (Boyd & Vandenberghe). Cost is negligible next to the fit, and a cheaper non-invariant check would lose the scale invariance that is the reason for the check.
- Batch AB.1: cluster-robust sandwich estimator: no_faster_acceptable_found. One hash-map pass for the score sums plus G rank-1 updates, O(np + Gp²). That is the cost of reading the data plus forming the meat. Nothing asymptotically or practically faster exists. Faster jackknife (CV3) methods change the estimator.
- Batch AB.1: HC0 sandwich estimator: no_faster_acceptable_found. Closed form, O(np² + p³). The catalog's (j) option already covers replacing solve() with chol2inv(chol()): about 2× on a p³ term that is negligible at p in the tens. Nothing new was found.
- Batch AB.1: HC2 sandwich estimator: no_faster_acceptable_found. Leverages via rowSums((XB)∘X) plus a weighted crossprod is O(np²), the minimum for exact leverages. Thin-QR leverages (catalog O13) cost more flops. Randomized or approximate leverage methods change the answer and only pay off at n far beyond EDI's.
- Batch AB.1: Hendricks–Koenker sandwich (nid): no_faster_acceptable_found. The quantreg ChangeLog shows no change to the nid/ker/iid SEs since 5.74, which lets nid reuse sfn. The fast new methods (conquer, ppro, pfnb, qfnb) are smoothed or large-n/many-τ methods: they change the estimator or only pay off far above EDI's n ≤ low thousands. The two extra rq fits at τ±h are inherent to nid. 'ker' (Powell) is a different estimator.
- Batch AB.1: pivoted QR inverse (chol2inv): no_faster_acceptable_found. Closed form: chol2inv on an R factor that already exists, O(p³) with p in the tens. Nothing faster.
- Batch AC.1: axis-by-axis exhaustive autotuning: no_faster_acceptable_found. Each axis has only a handful of candidates on a small family × n_grid. Bayesian optimization or interaction search would add model overhead with no clear win at that size. The real saving is fewer repetitions per cell, which is covered under the A/B acceptance rule (v1.0.5-TODO-12 paired analysis and racing).
- Batch AC.1: exhaustive A/B against incumbent: no_faster_acceptable_found. Racing (F-Race / irace), which drops losing candidates early, is already in the catalog §12 option (m) and tracked by v1.0.5-TODO-12. I found no new evidence that changes that verdict.
- Batch AC.1: greedy list scheduling (FIFO): no_faster_acceptable_found. LPT (bound 4/3 − 1/(3m) versus 2 − 1/m, O(n log n) sort) is already the Adopt verdict (v1.0.5-TODO-5) and is still not implemented: pending = seq_len(n_total) at inference_suite.R:1392. Work stealing or dynamic chunking adds nothing, because dispatch is already per task. Polling is fine: mccollect(wait = FALSE, timeout = 0.2) returns early when a child finishes.
- Batch AC.1: linear search (first win): no_faster_acceptable_found. The n_grid has 3–5 points, so bisection or galloping search gains nothing. The cost is dominated by repetitions per point.
- Batch AC.1: random-forest chained imputation (missRanger / missForest): no_faster_acceptable_found. Runs once for fixed designs. missRanger is already the fast chained-RF choice, with missForest as the slower fallback. The vignette's speed knobs (num.trees = 100, max.depth, min.node.size, fewer iterations) change the imputations, so they are estimator changes and not acceptable as a speedup. missRanger is GPL (≥ 2).

</details>

A finding on an algorithm not marked `faster_option_found`, or the reverse. Before `also_covers` existed, an agent that filed one finding for several sibling labels also lands here, so check the finding before acting on it.

192 sources cited; 1 carry a DOI. Hosts: cran.r-project.org 71, arxiv.org 49, en.wikipedia.org 10, libeigen.gitlab.io 6, github.com 4, ar5iv.labs.arxiv.org 4, ideas.repec.org 4, raw.githubusercontent.com 4, plato.asu.edu 3, stat.ethz.ch 3, degenerateconic.com 2, docs.scipy.org 2.
